Plan for the master script:
python

import zipfile, re, html, shutil, os
import datetime as dt
WD_XML_RPR = '<w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Times New Roman" w:hAnsi="Times New Roman"/><w:b w:val="false"/><w:i w:val="false"/><w:color w:val="000000"/><w:sz w:val="24"/></w:rPr>'
def esc(t): return t.replace('&','&amp;').replace('<','&lt;').replace('>','&gt;')
def get_tcpr(tc):
    m = re.search(r'<w:tcPr>.*?</w:tcPr>', tc, re.S)
    return m.group(0) if m else ''
def get_ppr(tc):
    m = re.search(r'<w:pPr>.*?</w:pPr>', tc, re.S)
    return m.group(0) if m else ''
def build_cell(tc, text):
    tcpr = get_tcpr(tc); ppr = get_ppr(tc)
    return ('<w:tc>' + tcpr + '<w:p>' + ppr + '<w:r>' + WD_XML_RPR +
            f'<w:t xml:space="preserve">{esc(text)}</w:t></w:r></w:p></w:tc>')
def cell_text(tc):
    paras = re.findall(r'<w:p[ >].*?</w:p>|<w:p/>', tc, re.S)
    out=[]
    for p in paras:
        runs = re.findall(r'<w:t(?: [^>]*)?>(.*?)</w:t>', p, re.S)
        t = html.unescape(''.join(runs)).strip()
        if t: out.append(t)
    return ' / '.join(out)
def split_rows(tbl):
    return re.findall(r'<w:tr[ >].*?</w:tr>|<w:tr/>', tbl, re.S)
def split_cells(row):
    return re.findall(r'<w:tc>.*?</w:tc>', row, re.S)

Next, per table:

    rows = split_rows(tbl)
    Identify the lesson rows: row index and original number (int of cell0 text).
    Build the list of lesson rows in order.
    Apply merges: for each (a, b, merged_topic) [a, b are original numbers]:
        Locate row_a, row_b.
        In row_a: replace cell1 with build_cell(cell1, merged_topic); replace cell2 with build_cell(cell2, '2'); set the date later (after all merges, assign dates to the surviving rows).
        Mark row_b for deletion.
    Delete the marked rows.
    Renumbering: for i, surviving_lesson_row: replace cell0 with build_cell(cell0, str(i+1)).
    Dates: assign to the surviving lesson rows in order: row.cell5 = build_cell(cell5, date_str).

Note the order of operations: after deleting rows, renumber, then set dates. All operations are on the row's XML string.

To replace a cell within a row's XML, I need to replace the specific <w:tc>...</w:tc> chunk. Since cells are non-overlapping, I can split the row into cells, modify the list, and rejoin. But a row also contains content that isn't a cell (the <w:tr ...> opening tag, attributes, possibly <w:trPr>). Let me preserve the opening part of the row: row_open = the part before the first <w:tc>, row_close = '</w:tr>'. Then new_row = row_open + ''.join(new_cells) + '</w:tr>'.

But wait: could there be other content between cells? In Word tables, a <w:tr> contains <w:trPr> (optional) followed by <w:tc> elements. So row_open includes <w:tr ...> + <w:trPr>...</w:trPr> (if present). Let me extract: first_tc_pos = row.find('<w:tc>'); row_open = row[:first_tc_pos]; the last cell ends with the final '</w:tc>'; row_close = row[last_tc_end:] (should be '</w:tr>'). Let me verify that row_close is exactly '</w:tr>'.

Actually, more simply: I'll use a function that replaces cell N in a row by splitting into cells and using a regex approach that handles the non-cell prefix/suffix.

Let me write:
python

def set_cell(row, idx, new_cell):
    cells = re.findall(r'<w:tc>.*?</w:tc>', row, re.S)
    # Rebuild by replacing the idx-th occurrence
    # Find the span of the idx-th cell
    spans = [m.span() for m in re.finditer(r'<w:tc>.*?</w:tc>', row, re.S)]
    s, e = spans[idx]
    return row[:s] + new_cell + row[e:]

This is clean — use finditer to get the exact span of the idx-th cell.

Next, for merging: get the cells of row_a, build new cell1 (topic) and cell2 (total=2), and set them. row_b is marked for deletion.

Renumbering: for surviving lesson rows in order (after deletion), set cell0 to str(new_number).

Dates: for surviving lesson rows in order, set cell5 to the date string.

But then the sequence is: row 96 (merged, 2 hours, date D), rows 97, 98 (1 hour each, dates D+1, D+2), row 100... Actually, deleting row 99 and keeping 96 means the order is preserved: 96 (2 hours), 97, 98, 100, 101, 102. This works — the merged row 96 covers both "певцы" (96) and "певец" (99). Pedagogically slightly odd (the "певец" content is taught at the position of row 96), but acceptable as a "combined date" approach.

Hmm, but actually — is it better to merge adjacent rows? For grade 11, the "певцы/певец" pair (96, 99) is a true duplicate but non-adjacent. Alternatively, I could merge the adjacent 96+97 (native-country певцы + country-being-studied писатели) — but those are different themes (not a duplicate), so merging them would be pedagogically worse. The user explicitly said duplicates can be merged. So 96+99 (the duplicate pair) is the right choice even if non-adjacent.

But wait — there's a subtlety with merging non-adjacent rows under the "combine under a single date" approach. If row 96 and row 99 get the same date, they'd be on the same day, but rows 97, 98 (in between) have different dates. That's impossible in a sequential schedule! In a sequential КТП (calendar-thematic plan), dates must be non-decreasing. Row 96 date=D, row 97 date=D+1, row 98 date=D+2, row 99 date=D (same as 96) — that's decreasing (D+2 then D). Invalid!

So merging non-adjacent rows under "one date" breaks the monotonic date sequence. I need to reconsider.

Options: (a) Only merge adjacent rows (so the combined row occupies one date and the sequence stays monotonic). (b) For non-adjacent duplicates, keep both rows (each with its own date) — but then they're not merged, and the lesson count doesn't decrease, so the deficit isn't resolved.

So I must pick adjacent merges that resolve the deficit. Let me redo the merge selection to use only adjacent pairs.

Let me reconsider each grade with adjacent-only merges.

So build_cell should take the original cell and, if it has an rPr, preserve it. Let me write get_rpr(tc) to find the first <w:rPr>.*?</w:rPr> in the cell. For empty cells (the date), it returns None, so I pass a fallback.

Let me implement:
python

def get_rpr(tc):
    m = re.search(r'<w:rPr>.*?</w:rPr>', tc, re.S)
    return m.group(0) if m else None
def build_cell(tc, text, rpr_fallback=None):
    tcpr = get_tcpr(tc); ppr = get_ppr(tc)
    rpr = get_rpr(tc) or rpr_fallback or DEFAULT_RPR
    return ('<w:tc>'+tcpr+'<w:p>'+ppr+'<w:r>'+rpr+
            f'<w:t xml:space="preserve">{esc(text)}</w:t></w:r></w:p></w:tc>')

DEFAULT_RPR = TNR sz24.

For the date cell: build_cell(date_cell, date_str, rpr_fallback=number_cell_rpr).

Let me get the number cell's rPr from the same row as fallback for the date.

first = tbl.find('<w:tr')
# But careful: '<w:tr' could also match '<w:trPr' or '<w:tr...' — need to find the first row element start.
m_first = re.search(r'<w:tr[ >]', tbl)
last_end = tbl.rfind('</w:tr>') + len('</w:tr>')
pre = tbl[:m_first.start()]
post = tbl[last_end:]
return pre + ''.join(newrows) + post, len(lesson_rows)
